| Tasks | Preserved the existing no-live-session filter; replaced the periodic timer with `ChildProcessPoller`, one shared request without overlap. Opening sessions does not advance deadlines. Defaults: 2500 ms after success, 10/20/40/60 s error backoff and warning on the third failure, automatic recovery; cancel the snapshot command only on session closure or disposal. Four validated central `childPolling` budgets. PowerShell uses terminating errors; empty or malformed Windows snapshots are errors. No CLM/AppLocker bypass. |
| Acceptance | With no live sessions, no `powershell.exe` from this polling starts within 30 seconds; hidden live sessions remain included to update their signals. With a live session the command does not start more often than the configured interval, 2500 ms today. After an error the next wait is at least 10 seconds and doubles up to 60 seconds. On the third consecutive error the state «detection unavailable» appears, in Italian and English, and the rest of the dashboard keeps working. Detection on paths already verified stays available when the command succeeds. |
| Required tests | Tests of the interval, the backoff (10 s, doubling, 60 s cap), the 30 seconds with no sessions and the message on the third error, with a fake command. Assess the `__PSLockdownPolicy=4` trial on Windows 11. AppLocker is a declared limit. Suite and build. Windows x64 smoke on the milestone release package, isolated profile. |
| Documentation | IT/EN manuals, `SECURITY_MODEL.md`, MAP, PLAN, version and the landing page if published behavior changes. |

Further centralization requires a new decision; P3 is the sole remaining open proposal, with its security/package portion already delivered in C9. Remaining proposals do not claim validation of untested platforms. Open point, package size only: do not exclude `node-pty` prebuilds for other platforms or the `.pdb` files. That is a future optimization, not part of C2. | Field | C27, formerly M27 — Packaged Electron fuses |
| --- | --- |
| Number and goal | C27, formerly M27 — R11-02. Turn off command-line Node execution in the distributed package and verify ASAR integrity. This is not PC-10 and it does not absorb M18. |
| Dependency and planned branch | After C9 and before M18; milestone completed. Historical branch `milestone/27-electron-fuses`, from main `5e85e757e15f1dc4a6f015a541b47a532e6a0577`. Started on 16 November 2026 at 13:08 (Europe/Rome). |
| Planned checkpoint and version | `milestone/C27`, `v0.3.8`; increment `+0.0.1` and a release, because the binary and the startup surface change. The `v0.3.18` alternative is withdrawn. |
| Tasks | Decided values: `runAsNode` false, `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` false, `enableNodeCliInspectArguments` false, `enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` true, `onlyLoadAppFromAsar` true, `enableCookieEncryption` false, `loadBrowserProcessSpecificV8Snapshot` false, `grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` true, `wasmTrapHandlers` true. All are declared, including Electron 45.3.2's ninth fuse (`EnableWasmTrapHandler`, index 8), which `@electron/fuses` 1.8.0 does not know. `resetAdHocDarwinSignature` true. `strictlyRequireAllFuses` true (I-11). electron-builder 26.18.0, through the `electronFuses` key, does not pass the ninth fuse or `strictlyRequireAllFuses`: the flip lives in an `afterPack` hook that imports `@electron/fuses` 2.1.3. `grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` stays true because the renderer is `file:` (R11-06). `asar.unpacked`, including `pty.node`, and `config` in extra resources stay outside ASAR integrity. Without signing, the check is defense in depth: whoever rewrites the binary can rewrite the fuse wire. Linux is not covered. `runAsNode` false does not change until the Windows test contradicts it (R12-03): `node-pty`, in `kill()`, uses `child_process.fork` for `conpty_console_list_agent` when `useConptyDll` is false, and `electron/sessions.ts` does not pass `useConptyDll`. If session close fails on the Windows package, the correction is `useConptyDll` or an alternative; a different fuse value goes back to Glauco before it is changed. |
| Acceptance | On the Windows x64 package and the macOS ARM64 package, `@electron/fuses` reads the nine decided values. `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` and `--inspect`/`--inspect-brk` do not start Node code in the release binary; on macOS neither does `SIGUSR1`. `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-http-header-size=8192` is the observable check: packaged Electron 45.3.2 keeps only `--http-parser` and `--max-http-header-size` in `NODE_OPTIONS` and drops `--inspect` and `--require` even when the `nodeOptions` fuse is on (`SetNodeOptions` in [shell/common/node_bindings.cc](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.3.2/shell/common/node_bindings.cc)). On the twin, `http.maxHeaderSize` is read through one connection to the node target of `--inspect=127.0.0.1:<port>`, via `process.getBuiltinModule('node:http')` because `require` is not defined in that context, then the socket is closed and the process is stopped; the value must be 8192. The read window is 12000 ms. On the release binary the same variable is ignored and stderr contains `NODE_OPTIONS ignored due to disabled nodeOptions fuse.` The ASAR has integrity checking on and the app loads only from the ASAR, with the files outside the ASAR named above. Release smoke does not use `--inspect-brk`. `--remote-debugging-port` and `-pipe` remain a documented residual risk (Vesta I-04 [Deduzione]): smoke accepts CDP only on `127.0.0.1` and does not use `-pipe`, which the binary still accepts. DevTools from an environment variable stay M18. |
| Required tests | Hybrid A+B, without path C. The twin is taken after electron-builder, from the finished unpacked directory, by resetting the fuses to their defaults: on Windows `rcedit` edits the exe after `afterPack`. The comparison, on macOS after `codesign --remove-signature`, allows differences only in the fuse wire. `runAsNode` false has no build-time condition: the ConPTY smoke of the same Windows workflow runs before the artifact upload. The full M9 smoke, including the main debugger, stays on that twin. On the release binary: fuse read, negative tests, reduced CDP smoke, and a byte-by-byte comparison limited to the fuse wire and, on macOS, the ad-hoc signature. I-09 is covered: `smoke-fuses.mjs` tries `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, `--inspect`, `--inspect-brk` and, on macOS, `SIGUSR1`. `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-http-header-size=8192` must apply on the twin and be ignored on the release binary. The portable 7z wrapper is not passed to `@electron/fuses`: fuses are read from the extracted `plancia-comandi.exe`, as in the `7z-nested` diagnostic; the unpacked exe and the installed exe are read directly. The `files.pty` trace follows node-pty's order (`build/Release`, `build/Debug`, `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>`): on Windows the package uses the prebuild, not `build/Release`. Each case writes, before rethrowing, a JSON line with the name, the binary (`release:` or `twin:` plus the file name), the outcome and the duration. Each case has its own HOME and APPDATA; `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` does not receive `--user-data-dir`. If the process exits before it stays up, the outcome is not `blocked`: the code, the signal and the stdout/stderr tail go into the message and into `smoke-failure.json`. Before the negative cases, `twin-startup` and `release-startup` launch the binary without `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` or `NODE_OPTIONS` and must stay up. The spawn profile inherits `process.env` and overrides only the isolation variables (`HOME`, `USERPROFILE`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `XDG_*`, `ZDOTDIR`). `PATH`, `SystemRoot`, `windir`, `TEMP` and `TMP` stay. On Windows, when the isolated profile has a drive letter, `HOMEDRIVE` and `HOMEPATH` follow that profile and, before spawn, `AppData\\Roaming` and `AppData\\Local` exist under `USERPROFILE`. Secret keys, `NODE_OPTIONS` and `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` are not inherited. Directories and `settings.json` are those of `smoke-package`, with `cwd` at the root and `windowsHide: false`. They do not set `ELECTRON_NO_ATTACH_CONSOLE`, `ELECTRON_ENABLE_LOGGING` or `--enable-logging`. `smoke-package`, with the debugger paused on the first line of `main.js`, calls `app.setPath` for `home`, `appData`, `userData` and `sessionData` before main calls `getPath`. With `PLANCIA_DIAG_STARTUP=1` main writes to stderr and to `startup.jsonl` the single-instance lock, the paths the process sees, whether preload, html, config and `pty.node` exist, the startup step, and the reason for `app.quit`, `app.exit`, exceptions and gone renderer or child processes. The `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection` handlers are registered only in that branch: without the variable, Electron's default behavior remains. If startup fails, the same file receives `startup-failed` with `step`, `name`, `message`, `stack`, `cause` (null when absent) and `code` (`UNKNOWN` when the exception has no code); `reportDiagnostic` stays limited to the event and code. Every `smoke-fuses` case includes `reap`. On Windows run `43900545314` the twin and the three variants exit at step `paths` with `Failed to get 'appData' path`; `release-startup` does not run; the twin's full smoke passes. [Deduction] on Windows `getPath('appData')` expands the known folder `%USERPROFILE%\\AppData\\Roaming` and fails when that directory is missing; the `APPDATA` variable is not the path that call reads, and `smoke-package` avoids the call with `setPath`. The same clean launch runs on the twin. Before every case the smoke closes leftover `plancia-comandi.exe` processes. On Windows and macOS a step that does not fail by itself prepares three copies of the unpacked package, with `enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation`, `onlyLoadAppFromAsar`, or both enabled on the twin, and records the launch in an artifact JSON. On Windows the same JSON says whether the release exe, the twin, the variants and, when they can be extracted, the portable and the installer contain the `INTEGRITY`/`ELECTRONASAR` resource, and whether the hash declared for `resources\\app.asar` matches the SHA-256 of the ASAR header read as `getRawHeader`. On Windows a step prints the release and twin fuses before smoke. `release` smoke accepts CDP only when the URL and the listener are `127.0.0.1`. SHA-256 chain from the tested file to the manifest and, once the asset is published, to the asset digest (I-05). The isolated-profile assertion (`boot.cwd` equal to the temporary root) stays on both paths (I-06). Coverage that leaves the main debugger (R12-02, I-07): `isolateBeforeMain`, the IPC/CSP probes through `main.evaluate` and the worker handshake under `--inspect-brk` stay on the twin. On the release binary, what remains is the environment set before spawn, the same `boot.cwd` assertion, renderer CDP and the negative fuse tests. On Windows, both the twin and the release binary open a ConPTY session, start `ping.exe` as a child, close the session and check that the shell, the child and any other child of the shell are gone, and that main stderr has no unhandled rejection or `fork` error (R12-03). During `kill()` it counts only `plancia-comandi.exe` processes whose command line has no `--type=`, excluding main, reports the count and fails if another appears: Electron helpers with `--type=` do not count. The package smoke turns on `PLANCIA_DIAG_STARTUP` and fails if `startup.jsonl` contains `uncaughtException` or `unhandledRejection`. On the portable build, CDP listeners are measured on the extracted main, the one without `--type=`, and on its helpers, not on the wrapper pid; if that process cannot be identified, or `netstat` returns no rows, the log says `portable CDP listeners not measured` and the measurement is not taken. Glauco chose A+C for M27: `runAsNode` stays off; on Windows spawn passes `useConptyDll: true` and, before `pty.kill()`, `taskkill /T /F` always closes the shell tree with `System32\taskkill.exe` and an argument array. `conpty.dll` and `OpenConsole.exe` stay in `app.asar.unpacked`, and inspection and smoke compare their SHA-256 with the checkout prebuild on the unpacked app, the portable app and the installed app. I-03 is POSTPONED to a milestone after M27, because this close is A+C for Windows terminals and does not run the byte test: one changed byte in `app.asar` must block startup; one changed byte in `pty.node`, inside `app.asar.unpacked`, is not covered by integrity. Vesta I-03, medium: “integrity covers only app.asar. app.asar.unpacked (pty.node, @xterm/headless) and config/app.config.json stay modifiable. Without a signature, the same user can roll the fuses and the digest back. Document it in SECURITY_MODEL as additional defense. Proposed test: change one byte in pty.node and one in app.asar.” Local suite and build. The Windows and macOS `workflow_dispatch` runs do not start with this kickoff: they are needed after the application commit, with that commit as `source_sha` and `version` `0.3.8`, from the ref that contains these scripts. |
| Documentation | `SECURITY_MODEL.md`, README, `ISTRUZIONI.md`, `INSTRUCTIONS.md`, MAP if scripts or workflows change, PLAN, release notes with platforms, limits and checksums. |
| Status | `C27`, formerly M27. Glauco's first approval on 16 November 2026 (t166u) is on commit `0e3b82ad985c56ffdf03025439a92a63cb8d1d6c`. Summary: packaged Electron fuses are closed and `runAsNode` is off; on Windows `useConptyDll: true` and `taskkill /PID /T /F` run through `execFile` without a shell before `pty.kill()`; `conpty.dll` and `OpenConsole.exe` are in `asarUnpack`, their SHA-256 is compared in `inspect-package`, and they are not entries of `SHA256SUMS-Windows.txt`; the `ping -t` child, orphan check and `ThreadCount` run only on win32, with a five-minute smoke budget; startup diagnostics run only with `PLANCIA_DIAG_STARTUP=1` and the written text is scrubbed. Verification at first review: Windows [43955392181](https://github.com/gloutchov/Plancia-Comandi/actions/runs/43955392181) and macOS [43955397389](https://github.com/gloutchov/Plancia-Comandi/actions/runs/43955397389) are green at `0e3b82ad985c56ffdf03025439a92a63cb8d1d6c`. Vulcano R12-04: “Add resetAdHocDarwinSignature: true for unsigned macOS arm64, list every fuse (including wasmTrapHandlers and loadBrowserProcessSpecificV8Snapshot) and declare @electron/fuses. Reconsider enableCookieEncryption, which is irreversible, requires a signature on macOS and gives little value with a file: renderer.” Resolved by Glauco's decision 2: cookie encryption false, all fuses declared. Vesta I-01, high: “add resetAdHocDarwinSignature: true. On macOS ARM64 with identity null, changing the fuses invalidates the ad-hoc signature and the app does not start [Deduzione].” Vesta I-02, medium: “enableCookieEncryption cannot be undone and on macOS wants a signed app, while the app does not use cookies. Proposal: false, or a test on a real Mac.” Resolved: false, Glauco's decision 2. Vesta I-08, alternatives to paths B and C: “Playwright _electron launches with --inspect, so it is incompatible [Deduzione]; a build flag used only for the test ends up in the binary; I propose a hybrid path A+B: full smoke on a twin build with the fuses open; on the release binary, reduced CDP smoke, fuses read and a byte-by-byte diff, which must differ only in the fuses and the signature.” Adopted, Glauco's decision 4. Vesta I-10 is on the M18 card. |

M16, M21, M22, M23 and M25 stay without a release and with the version unchanged. The other planned milestones keep the release. Published `docs/*` tags, including `docs/plan-vesta-baseline-2026-11-13`, stay on their commits and are historical. |
| Acceptance | `AGENTS.md`, `PLAN.md` and the Release section of `STARTUP_PREFERENCES_HOME.md` describe the same rule, in Italian and English where the file has both. The application version stays 0.3.1. No published tag is moved or deleted. |
| Required tests | `git diff --check`; check of the required documents, links and IT/EN equivalence. No application test. |
| Documentation | `PLAN.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `STARTUP_PREFERENCES_HOME.md`. |
| Status | `C26`, formerly M26. Glauco's first approval on 14 November 2026, at 11:17 (Europe/Rome). Tag `milestone/C26`. Version 0.3.1 unchanged, no release. |

### 3. Milestone conventions

- `P<n>`: optional proposal, not executable without an explicit designer decision.
- `M<n>`: planned milestone on a dedicated branch from `main`, after verification of the preceding checkpoint and a separate start request. Order: closed C29, closed C31, then closed C33 → closed C40 → closed C32 → closed C36 → closed and published unified C37, M38 absorbed (M41–M43 absorbed into C40; M39 removed; M34/M35 absorbed into C33; M30 into C29) as in 2.2, after closed C28. C18, formerly M18, retains number 18 for continuity with C27. Completed entries and historical tags do not change; earlier order exceptions remain history.
- `C<n>`: work approved by the designer, retaining its `M` number; publication and operational closure may still be pending.

`P` and `M` numbering is independent. Each future `M` must specify a functional goal, branch, `milestone/C<n>` checkpoint, version increment, tasks, acceptance criteria, tests, documentation and status. Do not start the next milestone until the preceding `main` and checkpoint are verified on the remote.

### 4. Closure checklist

Follow the complete procedure in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md). Copy and complete this checklist for every future milestone:

- [ ] Private GitHub identity checked; worktree and user changes preserved; previous checkpoint verified.
- [ ] Dedicated branch created; implementation, configuration and proportionate tests completed.
- [ ] Required automatic tests and smoke/manual checks run; omissions and limitations recorded.
- [ ] Version synchronized when required; no secrets or accidental tracked temporary files.
- [ ] README, IT/EN manuals and plan updated; security, map and operating rules updated when affected.
- [ ] Local commits created only on the dedicated branch; branch frozen with the entry still marked `M`.
- [ ] Hash, diff against `main`, tests, results and limitations presented; first personal review and explicit approval obtained.
- [ ] Only after first approval: `M` renamed to `C`, with summary, checks, limitations, version and checkpoint recorded in a new local commit.
- [ ] New hash and diff presented; second personal review and explicit approval obtained before any push, merge or tag.
- [ ] Branch/PR published only when needed; necessary CI verified before merging. Any fix receives new approval.
- [ ] Merge to `main`, push and remote commit verified; CI on `main` verified when necessary.
- [ ] Release artifacts and smoke tests, when required, and SHA-256 verified on the final commit before tagging.
- [ ] Annotated `milestone/C<n>` checkpoint and version tag if changed created and verified on the remote.
- [ ] Release required by the Release rule, when one is due, published and verified; obsolete branch removed only after verification and approval.
- [ ] Temporary paths presented for separate confirmation before cleanup; final worktree checked.

For a patch without an `M` entry, do not invent a milestone: freezing, final-commit review and approval apply, but the `M` → `C` transition, the second review caused by that transition and the milestone checkpoint do not. Any change after review requires new approval.

#### 4.13 M29 extension — stable pane selection

Glauco reported that the first fix tested with `npm run dev` works correctly. In the same session on 20 November 2026 he requested removing the clicked pane’s promotion to the primary position and highlighting the active one. This extension stays in M29/0.3.11; previously frozen commit `c3ccde5530d350de670cf51375290ac2eff39bce` is no longer the final commit for approval. No approval is presumed for subsequent changes.

- [x] Focus and group anchor separated in `App`. Clicking content, title or sidebar row of an already visible session selects only that session, without reordering the group. Side by side↔Stacked preserves pair, order and selection. Selecting a hidden session and Tile still bring it into view by explicitly choosing a group. ### 5. Versions, CI and distribution

Use `+0.0.1` for patches, `+0.1.0` for minor features and `+1.0.0` for major changes. Publication follows the single rule in the Release section of `STARTUP_PREFERENCES_HOME.md`, from 14 November 2026, with no project exception: a release only for changes that matter to how the app works. Documentation, CI, tests and refactoring with no effect have no release, leave the version unchanged and do not create a `vX.Y.Z` tag. If the app is not usable, a release that is due is marked as a prerelease. Do not configure signing, notarization or automatic updates without explicit credentials and a process.

The `package-windows.yml` workflow is manually triggered for a Windows build attempt that cannot be performed equivalently locally. It does not run on push or PR. Run `43226304320` produced the Windows packages of release v0.3.0 and also checked the portable executable and the installed app. The `package-macos.yml` workflow is manual only, on the `macos-15` runner, for unsigned ARM64 DMG and ZIP files; it does not run on push or PR. It ran as `43257940568` and the artifacts are in release [v0.3.0](https://github.com/gloutchov/Plancia-Comandi/releases/tag/v0.3.0). The M2 closing plan approved on 12 November 2026 includes this reproducible remote macOS build for the v0.3.0 release assets, with SHA-256, alongside the Windows workflow: it spends minutes only when launched. The macOS ARM64 checks for release `v0.2.0` run locally. Additional workflows require a separate justification. `npm run build` alone does not establish that a package has been verified. `.github/dependabot.yml` (23 November 2026) is not a workflow and does not run on push or pull request. After publication on `main`, the Dependabot service opens the version updates.
